Let me plan the proof. This is the classic STLC strong normalization proof with locally nameless representation, following the Software Foundations "StlcProp" / "Norm" chapter approach.

The standard SF approach for strong normalization uses a logical relation indexed by type:
- `R T t` := `strongly_normalizing t` roughly, plus a notion of "step-preservation"/"closed under expansion".

Actually, in SF's Norm chapter (for STLC with CBV), the logical relation is defined as:

```coq
Fixpoint A (T : ty) : tm -> Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => fun t => strongly_normalizing t
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => fun t => 
      strongly_normalizing t /\ 
      (forall t', step t t' -> A (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) t') /\
      (forall u, A T1 u -> A T2 (tm_app t u))
  end.
```

Hmm, but with locally nameless we need to deal with free variables. Let me think about how to handle the locally nameless part.

In locally nameless STLC (SF's Stlc with LN), the standard approach for preservation etc. uses the "open with fvar" pattern and `lc_at`. For normalization, the key challenge: T_Abs gives us `forall x not in L, has_type (x |-> T1; Gamma) (open t1 (fvar x)) T2`. We need to show the body (abstracted) is in the relation. So we need some notion of the relation at "open" terms: define a relation on terms with free variables via a context substitution interpretation or via well-formedness.

A common approach for LN: define the logical relation on *closed* terms only... but the lambda body is not closed. Alternative: use "the relation on open terms through valid opening" — i.e., define `R T t` for terms possibly with free variables by quantifying over substitutions: `forall (sigma : atom -> tm) ...`.

Alternatively, we can use the standard SF Norm approach with named variables. Here we have locally nameless, so we need to adapt.

Let me think about the cleanest way given the language: no recursion in the language, so the relation approach works.

Approach: Define semantic typing for open terms via a "term substitution" interpretation with respect to a context. Actually a simpler approach that avoids substitution: define a logical relation `R T t` for terms with free variables, where free variables are handled by an interpretation of the environment that maps each free variable to a value of its type plus a property that the term is "locally closed" relative to that.

Hmm. Actually, let me recall that these benchmark tasks (STLC normalization with LN) come from a known family. Let me think about the standard solution.

The cleanest way in locally nameless: define a relation on terms with free variables parameterized by a "semantic context" — a function from atoms to *sets of terms* (the candidate values for each fvar). Then define:

`R T t` (for a fixed semantic context `rhos : atom -> tm -> Prop`) meaning: for every closing substitution `sigma : atom -> tm` such that for each x, `rhos x (sigma x)`, we have `R_closed T (sigma t)`, where `sigma t` is the simultaneous substitution.

But we need substitution machinery: `subst` replacing free variables. That's straightforward to define (a fixpoint on tm replacing fvars).

Actually we could avoid substitution at the top-level by working with "opening" and using the fact that the body opened with a fvar, then quantifying over candidate terms.

The key property we need is "expansion closure" (also called "R is closed under reduction / backwards reduction"): if `t` is SN and all one-step reducts of `t` are in R T, then `t` is in R T. This is needed for the compatibility lemma for application: `R (T1->T2) t1 -> R T1 t2 -> R T2 (t1 t2)`, where t1's value case is a lambda, and we need to handle the step relation.

Honestly, rather than recall, let me design a clean, self-contained proof. I have full control; let me think carefully about what's easiest in Rocq with the LN representation.

**Strategy**.

Given the complications with LN (free variables in lambda bodies), I think the cleanest approach is:

Define a logical relation on terms with free variables, parameterized by a mapping from atoms to terms (a closing substitution). Actually, better: use the approach of "R T t" for open terms defined via semantic contexts where free variables are interpreted as related terms:

```
Definition sem_env := atom -> tm.   (* actually atom -> tm: substitution *)

(* Substitution of free variables *)
Fixpoint subst (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => sigma x
  | ...
  end.
```

Then define:
```
Fixpoint R (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => SN t
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      SN t /\
      (forall t', t --> t' -> R (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) t') /\
      (forall v, value v -> R T1 v -> R T2 (tm_app t v))
  end.
```
on *closed-ish* terms, and then define the open interpretation:
```
Definition R_ctx (Gamma : context) (t : tm) (T : ty) : Prop :=
  forall sigma, (forall x S, Gamma x = Some S -> R S (sigma x)) -> R T (subst sigma t).
```

This is the standard "semantic typing" interpretation. Then:

Actually, there's a classic simpler approach for CBV SN: define the relation at arrow type to include:

Note the second conjunct: step-closure as part of the definition. This makes SN (app t1 t2) easier:

```coq
Lemma R_app : forall T1 T2 t1 t2,
    R (TArrow T1 T2) t1 ->
```

```coq
Fixpoint R (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | TBool => strongly_normalizing t
  | TArrow T1 T2 => 
      strongly_normalizing t /\
      (forall t', step t t' -> R (TArrow T1 T2) t') /\
      (forall u, R T1 u -> R T2 (tapp t u))
```

**Proving SN (app t1 t2)**. We have SN t1 and SN t2, and (for t1 value) the property P: forall v, value v -> R T1 v -> R T2 (app t1 v).

Standard: define the predicate on t1: `Q t1 := forall t2, SN t2 -> R T1 t2 -> (forall v, value v -> R T1 v -> R T2 (app t1 v)) -> SN (app t1 t2)`. Prove Q t1 by induction on SN t1 (i.e., using SN's induction principle: SN t1 -> (forall t1', t1 --> t1' -> Q t1') -> Q t1). Hmm, but SN's induction principle: `strongly_normalizing t` is an inductive with constructor SN_intro (forall t', t --> t' -> SN t') -> SN t. The induction principle gives: (forall t, (forall t', t --> t' -> P t') -> P t) -> forall t, SN t -> P t. Yes.

For this we need at minimum SN (app t1 t2) — which is "the fundamental theorem for applications" — and this is essentially the crux of strong normalization for STLC. The classical Tait/Girard proof does exactly: R at arrow includes closure under application, and the proof of the fundamental theorem's application case is proven *simultaneously* with the definitional properties by induction on types, using the fact that R T is "closed under reduction" or by defining R via SN and app-closure and proving everything by induction on types with a key lemma.

Let me think about the classical proof more carefully:

Define:
- `R Bool t := SN t`
- `R (T1->T2) t := SN t /\ forall v, value v -> R T1 v -> R T2 (app t v)`

Key lemmas:
(L1) R T t -> SN t (immediate, by induction on T).
(L2) R T t -> t --> t' -> R T t' (step closure) — induction on T; arrow case needs lc v (so include lc or prove values are lc).
(L3) R T t -> t -->* t' -> R T t' maybe.
(L4) For t with SN t, if all one-step reducts satisfy R T, then R T t? Not needed maybe. Hmm.
(L5) R_app: R (T1->T2) t1 -> R T1 t2 -> R T2 (app t1 t2).

For L5 with T2 = T2a -> T2b, the app-closure conjunct requires: forall v value in R T2a, R T2b (app (app t1 t2) v). Now, here's the trick: prove a stronger/more general form:

**Generalized app lemma**: `R (T1 -> T2) t1 -> forall u, R T1 u -> R T2 (app t1 u)` where the proof goes by induction on the *SN derivation of t1 and u*... but the app-closure conjunct is about app (app t1 u) v which is a *different* term.

Alternatively, note: for the app-closure conjunct at T2, we need `R T2b (app (app t1 t2) v)`. Since T2b is a subterm of T2, maybe we can prove the app lemma by induction on T2 where in the arrow case we use the IH at T2b, which needs R (T2a -> T2b) (app t1 t2) — the goal itself but... at the same type. Circular indeed.

Hmm, unless we prove the app lemma at T2 *by induction on the SN derivation* of `app t1 t2`? To show R T2 (app t1 t2) with T2 = T2a->T2b, the app-closure conjunct requires R T2b (app (app t1 t2) v) — and we could use the SN-based induction: app (app t1 t2) v is a bigger term, not a reduct. Hmm, no.

OK, the real classical solution: **Kripke-style / Girard's "candidates of reducibility" with a saturated-set structure**. Define R as the smallest set closed under certain rules (inductively), or define R T as a "saturated set": a set S of terms such that (a) S ⊆ SN, (b) if t is SN and all reducts in S then t ∈ S (or: if t reduces to something in S...). Then the arrow type's candidate is defined as {t | SN t and forall u ∈ A, app t u ∈ B}. This is not defined by a Fixpoint but as a predicate on sets. Then the key lemma is: the candidate at each type is saturated, and the fundamental theorem is proved by induction on typing derivations with the induction hypothesis giving candidates for subterms. The problematic app-closure conjunct disappears because R is a *set* defined via SN + app closure, and saturation is proved by induction on types.

Wait, but the app-closure issue for R T2 (app t1 t2) where T2 is an arrow: In the saturated-set approach, `R T2 (app t1 t2)` means app t1 t2 ∈ Candidate(T2) = {t | SN t and forall u ∈ Candidate(T2a), app t u ∈ Candidate(T2b)}. To prove this we need: for all u ∈ Cand(T2a), app (app t1 t2) u ∈ Cand(T2b). And indeed we would prove this using t1's membership: t1 ∈ Cand(T1->T2) means forall u ∈ Cand(T1), app t1 u ∈ Cand(T2) — using u := t2 doesn't work since t2 ∈ Cand(T1) is exactly what we have! Wait, YES: t1 ∈ Cand(T1 -> T2) means (forall u, u ∈ Cand(T1) -> app t1 u ∈ Cand(T2)). Taking u := t2 (which is in Cand(T1)) gives app t1 t2 ∈ Cand(T2) — that's exactly the goal! So in the saturated-set formulation, R at arrow *already contains* the universal app-closure over all related arguments, and the "app lemma" is just applying this conjunct. The issue in my Fixpoint formulation was requiring `value v` in the app-closure conjunct; dropping the `value` requirement makes the app lemma trivial (it's just the definitional conjunct).

But then, how do we prove that `abs T b` is in R (T1->T2)? We'd need: forall u, R T1 u -> R T2 (app (abs T b) u) = R T2 (open b u)?? For arbitrary u ∈ R T1 (not necessarily a value), `app (abs T b) u` does NOT step to `open b u` (CBV needs u to be a value). So we can't just use IH at u. Hmm. So we need to show app (abs T b) u ∈ R T2 for arbitrary u ∈ R T1, not just values.

That's where saturation helps: to show app (abs T b) u ∈ R T2, use the saturation property of R T2: it suffices to show SN (app (abs T b) u) and that all one-step reducts are in R T2. The reducts: (a) if u is a value: open b u — shown via IH; (b) if u --> u': app (abs T b) u' — apply saturation/induction on SN u'? Here u' ∈ R T1 by step closure, and u' is a smaller SN term, so induction on SN u works: "for all u ∈ R T1, app (abs T b) u ∈ R T2" by induction on SN u (which we have since u ∈ R T1 → SN u). This works!

So the definition should be:
```
R Bool t := SN t
R (T1->T2) t := SN t /\ (forall u, R T1 u -> R T2 (app t u))
```
Wait, but then the app-closure conjunct for R T2 (app t1 t2) — with this definition R (T1->T2) t := SN t /\ forall u, R T1 u -> R T2 (app t u), and T_App case: from R (T1->T2) t1 we directly get forall u, R T1 u -> R T2 (app t1 u), apply to t2. ✓ Trivial!

Now the abs case: prove R (T1->T2) (abs T1 b) given IH : forall x fresh, R T2 (open... ) etc:
- SN (abs T1 b) ✓ (value).
- forall u, R T1 u -> R T2 (app (abs T1 b) u). Proof by induction on SN u (from R T1 u we get SN u; need R_sn lemma). 
  - SN (app (abs T1 b) u): SN_app-like again... hmm, we still need SN of the application.
  - R T2 (app (abs T1 b) u) overall.

So we need: `R (T1->T2) (abs T1 b)` ⟸ (forall v, value v -> R T1 v -> R T2 (open b v)) — the value-restricted app closure implies the full app closure. That's the "saturation/expansion" argument. It requires proving R T2 (app (abs T1 b) u) for arbitrary u ∈ R T1, by induction on SN u, showing all reducts of app (abs T1 b) u are in R T2 and it's SN.

So we need a lemma like: "R is closed under expansion": if SN t and all one-step reducts of t are in R T, then t ∈ R T. Let's call it `R_expand` or `R_sn_step`. Is this provable by induction on T?

- Bool: R Bool t = SN t; need SN t, which is given. ✓ (doesn't even need the reducts).
- Arrow: need SN t ✓ and forall u, R T1 u -> R T2 (app t u). So for arbitrary u ∈ R T1, need R T2 (app t u). Use IH at T2 with t' := app t u: need SN (app t u) and all one-step reducts of app t u in R T2.
  - SN (app t u): from SN t and SN u — this is the SN_app_aux argument we designed, using the hypothesis "all reducts of t are in R T". Specifically: steps of app t u:
    - ST_App1: t --> t', reduct app t' u; R (arrow) t' from hypothesis (all reducts of t in R (T1->T2)); then its app-closure conjunct (now over ALL u, not just values!) gives R T2 (app t' u) directly. ✓ Then SN via R_sn at T2.
    - ST_App2: value t, u --> u', reduct app t u'; R T1 u' from R_step at T1; then we need R T2 (app t u')... by inner induction on SN u? Let me structure this as: to prove `forall u, R T1 u -> R T2 (app t u)` (call it goal G(t)) given SN t and (forall t', t --> t', R (T1->T2) t'), we do induction on SN u, and for each u we need R T2 (app t u). Yes! Induction on the type: Let's define the induction hypothesis as: for all smaller types, R_step and R_expand hold. Let me carefully structure the whole proof as: We prove by induction on type T (outer), a bunch of properties of R T. Actually, here's a cleaner classic structure:

**Properties of R, proven by induction on T:**
1. `R_sn : R T t -> SN t`.
2. `R_step : R T t -> t --> t' -> R T t'`.
3. `R_expand`: SN t -> (forall t', t --> t' -> R T t') -> R T t.
4. Maybe `R_value`: value-related properties.

Hmm, actually let me look at this from a different angle. There's a very well-known clean presentation in "Logical Foundations" / "Programming Language Foundations" Norm.v. Let me try to recall it properly, because those proofs are known to work. I believe the SF Norm.v definition is:

```coq
Fixpoint R (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | TBool => strongly_normalizing t
  | TArrow T1 T2 => 
      strongly_normalizing t /\
      (forall t', step t t' -> R (TArrow T1 T2) t') /\
      (forall u, R T1 u -> R T2 (tapp t u))
  end.
```

and the key lemma is:

```coq
Lemma R_app : forall T1 T2 (t1 t2 : tm),
  R (TArrow T1 T2) t1 ->
  R T1 t2 ->
  R T2 (tapp t1 t2).
Proof.
  intros T1 T2 t1 t2 H1 H2. 
  (* SN t1 and the app-closure *)
  ...
```

Hmm, actually a "saturated set" approach: A set S of terms is saturated if:
- S ⊆ SN
- for every term t: if SN t and every one-step reduct of t is in S, then t ∈ S.  (equivalently CR1+CR3)

Define R T as a predicate on terms by recursion on T:
- R Bool t := SN t
- R (T1->T2) t := SN t /\ forall u, R T1 u -> R T2 (app t u)

Then prove by induction on T:
- (K1) R T t -> SN t.
- (K2) R T t -> t --> t' -> R T t'.
- (K3) [saturation] SN t -> (forall t', t --> t' -> R T t') -> R T t.
- (K4) [app] R (T1->T2) t -> R T1 u -> R T2 (app t u)  — trivial by definition.
- (K5) [abs] (forall u, R T1 u -> R T2 (open b u)) -> R (T1->T2) (abs T1 b)? Hmm, need `forall u ∈ R T1, R T2 (app (abs T1 b) u)`; but for non-value u, app (abs T1 b) u doesn't step to open b u directly. Use K3 at T2: show SN (app (abs T1 b) u) and all reducts in R T2.

```
Definition SN := strongly_normalizing.

Fixpoint R (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => locally_closed t /\ SN t
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => locally_closed t /\ SN t /\ (forall u, R T1 u -> R T2 (tm_app t u))
  end.
```

Hmm, hmm. The app-closure conjunct in R at arrow makes things complicated because it asks for closure under application of arbitrary related arguments, and to prove it for a term like `app (abs b) u` we need a double induction.

Standard resolution: prove the app lemma by **induction on T2** (inner) and simultaneously something. Let's think: `P(T2) := forall T1 t1 t2, R (T1->T2) t1 -> R T1 t2 -> R T2 (app t1 t2)`. Alternative: use the **value-based definition** and prove app-closure by a *different* induction: the "SN of t1 and t2" induction where the circular (c) conjunct is handled by noticing that `R T2 (app t1 t2)`'s conjunct (c) is about a term whose SN derivation... hmm.

Honestly, the saturated-set approach is the standard solution precisely because it avoids this. In the saturated set approach, R T is not defined by a Fixpoint with app-closure; instead, candidates are sets satisfying saturation, and the arrow candidate is defined as {t | SN t ∧ ∀u ∈ A. app t u ∈ B}. The "circularity" in proving the app lemma vanishes because the app lemma IS the definitional property (forall u ∈ A, app t u ∈ B). And the abs lemma needs saturation: to show abs T1 b ∈ (A ⇒ B), need app (abs T1 b) u ∈ B for all u ∈ A. This is proven by induction on SN u (plus the value-closure from the fundamental theorem IH). Let me check:
- For u ∈ A (so SN u), show M(u) := app (abs T1 b) u ∈ B.
- B is a candidate (saturated set with SN property). To show app (abs T1 b) u ∈ B, use B's saturation property: SN (app (abs T1 b) u) and all reducts in B.
  - Reducts: ST_AppAbs (u = v value): open b v ∈ B by the IH hypothesis (forall v value ∈ A, open b v ∈ B). ST_App2: app (abs T1 b) u' with u --> u' — use M(u') from the inner induction on SN u (u' ∈ A by A's saturation/step closure).
  - SN (app (abs T1 b) u): by SN_intro with the same case analysis: for ST_AppAbs use open b v ∈ B → SN (B's SN property); for ST_App2 use M(u') → SN. 
  - So for B being any saturated set, this works. But saturation requires: SN t ∧ (all reducts in B) ⟹ t ∈ B. That's the definition of saturated (CR3).
  - The "app-closure" property at T2 for the term `app (abs T1 b) u` is automatically handled because B's saturation is a *property of the set*, proven once for all sets by induction on the type. 

So the saturated-set approach's key advantage: R T is a *set* (predicate) defined compositionally, and the crucial lemma "R T is saturated" is proven by induction on T. Let me formalize:

```
Definition saturated (S : tm -> Prop) : Prop :=
  (forall t, S t -> SN t) /\
  (forall t, SN t -> (forall t', step t t' -> S t') -> S t).
```

  Indeed, saturation (CR3) is NOT a property of the arrow candidate when defined as above; the correct CR3 formulation is for *neutral* terms. The standard Girard definition: CR1 (SN), CR2 (closed under reduction), CR3 (if t is neutral and all its one-step reducts are in S then t ∈ S). The abs case is then handled separately by the "abstraction lemma" which uses the value-based app-closure... but our arrow candidate is defined with app-closure over ALL related u, and we need to show that abs b ∈ candidate given "forall v value ∈ A, open b v ∈ B".     So: define neutral t := not a value (or "t is an application"). CR3: neutral t → SN t → (all reducts in S) → S t. And the abs lemma: for all u ∈ A, app (abs b) u ∈ B by induction on SN u:
      - app (abs b) u is neutral ✓ (an application can't be a value... in this language values are abs/true/false; tm_app is never a value ✓).
      - SN (app (abs b) u): by SN_intro using reducts analysis: ST_AppAbs → open b v ∈ B → SN; ST_App2 → app (abs b) u' with IH (u' ∈ A via CR2 of A) → SN.
      - all reducts in B: same analysis ✓.
      So by CR3 of B: app (abs b) u ∈ B ✓.

    - Arrow candidate C = {t | SN t ∧ ∀u ∈ A, app t u ∈ B}.

      So CR3 for arrow works when t is neutral. 

So the plan: define the candidate family with lc + SN + app-closure, and prove by induction on type T:
  (1) `R_lc : R T t -> locally_closed t`
  (2) `R_sn : R T t -> SN t`
  (3) `R_step : R T t -> t --> t' -> R T t'`  (CR2)

Let me now define things concretely and write the proof.

Definitions:

```coq
Definition SN := strongly_normalizing.

Definition neutral (t : tm) : Prop :=
  (forall T b, t <> tm_abs T b) /\ t <> tm_true /\ t <> tm_false.
```
Or simpler: `Definition value_or ... ` Hmm. Let's define:
```
Definition neutral (t : tm) : Prop := ~ value t.
```
That's simplest! Then neutral t means not a value, ruling out abs/true/false ✓. And `app x y` is never a value ✓ (need lemma: neutral (app x y), by intro; inversion on value; inversion on the equalities).

Let me define:
```
Definition neutral (t : tm) : Prop := ~ value t.
```

Now the relation:
```
Fixpoint R (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => locally_closed t /\ SN t
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      locally_closed t /\ SN t /\ (forall u, R T1 u -> R T2 (tm_app t u))
  end.
```

And the environment interpretation:
```
Definition R_env (sigma : atom -> tm) (Gamma : context) : Prop :=
  forall x T, Gamma x = Some T -> R T (sigma x).

Definition R_ctx (Gamma : context) (t : tm) (T : ty) : Prop :=
  forall sigma, R_env sigma Gamma -> R T (subst sigma t).
```

Lemmas (all by induction on T unless noted):

L1 `R_lc : forall T t, R T t -> locally_closed t` — destruct T; arrow: first conjunct; bool: first conjunct.

L2 `R_sn : forall T t, R T t -> SN t` — bool: second conjunct; arrow: second conjunct.

L3 `R_step : forall T t t', R T t -> step t t' -> R T t'` — induction on T.
  - Bool: need lc t' and SN t'. lc t' from lc_preservation step. SN t' from SN t and step: `SN_step : SN t -> step t t' -> SN t'` by inversion on SN.
  - Arrow T1 T2: need lc t', SN t', and forall u, R T1 u -> R T2 (app t' u).
    - lc t' from step preservation ✓.
    - SN t' from SN_step ✓.
    - For u with R T1 u: we have R T2 (app t u) from H's third conjunct. Need R T2 (app t' u); apply IH at T2 to the step `app t u --> app t' u` (ST_App1 with lc u from R_lc at T1). ✓ (IH T2 : forall t t', R T2 t -> step t t' -> R T2 t').

L4 `R_neutral : forall T t, locally_closed t -> neutral t -> SN t -> (forall t', step t t' -> R T t') -> R T t` — induction on T.
  - Bool: lc ∧ SN ✓.
  - Arrow T1 T2: lc ✓, SN ✓, forall u, R T1 u -> R T2 (app t u).
    Given u with Hu : R T1 u. Need R T2 (app t u). Use IH at T2 (i.e., R_neutral at T2) with:
      - lc (app t u) ✓ from lc t, R_lc at T1.
      - neutral (app t u): app is never a value ✓.
      - SN (app t u): prove by SN_intro: for all t'', step (app t u) t'' -> SN t''. Cases:
        - ST_App1 t t' Hstep : t'' = app t' u. We have R (T1->T2) t' from Hreds t t' Hstep; its app-closure conjunct gives R T2 (app t' u); R_sn gives SN ✓.
        - ST_App2 v t2 t2' Hvalue Hstep: here v = t, t2 = u, t'' = app t u'. Need SN (app t u'). Hmm, we need R T2 (app t u') then R_sn. But we're in the middle of proving R T2 (app t u) via R_neutral at T2, whose reduct hypothesis is "forall t'', step (app t u) t'' -> R T2 t''" — we need to provide it. So we need R T2 (app t u') for u' a reduct of u. That comes from... the inner induction on SN u! So the proof of the reduct hypothesis for `app t u` needs an inner induction on SN u (since u ∈ R T1 → SN u). 
        
        So the structure of L4's arrow case: to prove forall u, R T1 u -> R T2 (app t u), do induction on SN u:
        ```
        assert (H : forall u, R T1 u -> R T2 (app t u)).
        { intros u Hu. 
          induction (R_sn T1 u Hu) as [u Hu_step IH_u].  (* SN u induction *)
          ...
        }
        ```
        Hmm, but `R_sn T1 u Hu` is a term of type SN u; we can do `remember`... In Rocq: `pose proof (R_sn _ _ Hu) as Hsn`. Then `revert Hu; induction Hsn; intros Hu`? Hmm, careful: Hu : R T1 u depends on u. Pattern:
        ```
        intros u Hu.
        pose proof (R_sn T1 u Hu) as Hsn.
        revert Hu.
        induction Hsn as [u Hstep_u IH_u]; intros Hu.
        ```
        Wait, `induction Hsn` generalizes u in the goal. The goal is `R T2 (app t u)`; after reverting Hu, context has Hsn : SN u... Actually `pose proof` creates a hypothesis Hsn : SN u where u is the variable. `revert Hu` then `induction Hsn` — Coq needs to abstract u; Hsn : SN u is the thing being inducted, so `induction Hsn` will abstract u and give motive fun u => R T2 (app t u) (with Hu reverted). Then in the step case, IH_u : forall u', step u u' -> R T2 (app t u')... but wait, we also need R T1 u' for u'. We can get it from R_step at T1 applied to Hu and the step.         Hmm, careful: in the ST_App2 case for the reduct hypothesis, we need R T1 u' — that's L3 at T1 = R_step with type T1, which requires induction on T1... but we're inside the induction on T (the type) for L4; T1 and T2 are subterms of T = T1 -> T2, so IH_T1 and IH_T2 are available ✓ (R_step needs to be proven before, separately — fine, L3 is proven by induction on T independent of L4).

And the fundamental theorem:
```
Theorem fundamental : forall Gamma t T, has_type Gamma t T -> R_ctx Gamma t T.
Proof.
  induction 1; intros sigma Hsigma; simpl.
  - (* T_Var *) apply Hsigma in H. exact H.   (* subst sigma (fvar x) = sigma x *)
  - (* T_Abs *) 
      (* goal: R (T1->T2) (subst sigma (tm_abs T1 t1)) *)
      simpl. apply R_abs.
      + lc: need locally_closed (tm_abs T1 (subst sigma t1)).
        From has_type → lc (abs T1 t1) → lc_at 1 t1. Then subst preserves lc_at: lc_at 1 (subst sigma t1), using lc_at 0 (sigma y) for all y (from R_env → R → lc) and monotonicity.
      + forall v, value v -> R T1 v -> R T2 (open (subst sigma t1) v):
        pick x fresh from L and fv t1.
        Have IH : forall x, ~ In x L -> forall sigma, R_env sigma (update Gamma x T1) -> R T2 (subst sigma (open t1 (fvar x))).
        Let sigma' := upd sigma x v.
        R_env sigma' (update Gamma x T1) — prove via update lemmas.
        Get R T2 (subst sigma' (open t1 (fvar x))).
        Rewrite commutation: subst sigma' (open t1 (fvar x)) = open (subst sigma t1) v. 
        Actually: subst sigma' (open_rec 0 (fvar x) t1) = open_rec 0 (subst sigma' (fvar x)) (subst sigma' t1) = open_rec 0 v (subst sigma t1) [using freshness x ∉ fv t1].
        ✓
  - (* T_App *) simpl. apply R_app with T1; [IH1 sigma Hsigma | IH2 sigma Hsigma].
  - (* T_True *) simpl. split; [lc_true | SN_true].
  - (* T_False *) similar.
  - (* T_If *) simpl. Need R T (if (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2) (subst sigma t3)) from
      IH1 : forall sigma, R_env sigma Gamma -> R Bool (subst sigma t1), etc.
      Use a lemma R_if : R Bool b -> R T u -> R T w -> R T (if b u w), proven via R_neutral at T:
        - lc (if b u w) ✓
        - neutral ✓
        - SN (if b u w): induction on SN b, with reducts analysis:
          - ST_IfTrue: b = true, reduct u → R_sn ✓
          - ST_IfFalse: b = false, reduct w ✓

Since R_if is being proven by induction on SN b, the IH applies to b' with step b b'. But careful: the IH is on SN b; b' has SN b' from SN_step. So structure:
          ```
          Lemma R_if : forall T b u w, R Bool b -> R T u -> R T w -> R T (if b u w).
          Proof.
            intros T b u w Hb Hu Hw.
            pose proof (R_sn _ _ Hb) as Hsn.
            revert Hb Hu Hw? 
            induction Hsn as [b Hstep_b IH]; intros Hb Hu Hw.
            apply R_neutral with T; [lc | neutral | SN | reducts].
            - reducts t'': step (if b u w) t'' -> R T t'':
              * ST_IfTrue (b = true): t'' = u ✓ Hu.
              * ST_IfFalse: t'' = w ✓ Hw.
              * ST_If: b --> b', t'' = if b' u w; apply IH with (R Bool b') (via R_step at Bool) Hu Hw ✓.
              * Wait, also ST_If's other premises.

          ```
          Hmm, the IH from `induction Hsn` : forall b', step b b' -> R Bool b' -> R T u -> R T w -> R T (if b' u w). Then the main theorem:
```
```
Theorem normalization : forall t T, empty |-- t \in T -> SN t.
Proof.
  intros t T Hty.
  assert (HR : R T t) by (apply (fundamental empty t T Hty (fun x => tm_fvar x)); intros x S HS; inversion HS (* empty x = None *)).
  apply R_sn in HR; exact HR.
Qed.
```
Wait: R_ctx empty t T = forall sigma, R_env sigma empty -> R T (subst sigma t). Take sigma := fun x => tm_fvar x, so subst sigma t = t (needs lemma subst_id_fvar : subst (fun x => fvar x) t = t). And R_env sigma empty is vacuous since empty x = None. ✓

So I need `subst_fvar_identity : forall t, subst (fun x => tm_fvar x) t = t`.

**Substitution:**
```coq
Definition subst (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with ... Fixpoint
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => sigma x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst sigma t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => tm_if (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2) (subst sigma t3)
  end.
```

Lemmas:
- `subst_open : forall sigma k u t, subst sigma (open_rec k u t) = open_rec k (subst sigma u) (subst sigma t)`. Induction on t.
- `subst_fvar_id : forall t, subst (fun x => tm_fvar x) t = t`. Induction on t.
- `fv : tm -> list atom` and `fv_spec`? For the freshness lemma:
  - `subst_upd_agree : forall t x v sigma, ~ In x (fv t) -> subst (upd sigma x v) t = subst sigma t`. Induction on t.
  Actually we need for the commutation: `subst sigma' (open t1 (fvar x))` where sigma' = upd sigma x v. Using subst_open: = open_rec 0 (subst sigma' (fvar x)) (subst sigma' t1) = open_rec 0 v (subst sigma' t1) [since subst sigma' (fvar x) = sigma' x = v if eqb x x = true ✓]. Then with `~ In x (fv t1)`: subst sigma' t1 = subst sigma t1. So the lemma needed is exactly `subst_upd_agree`. ✓
- Also `subst_lc_at : forall sigma t k, (forall x, lc_at k (sigma x)) -> lc_at k t -> lc_at k (subst sigma t)`. Induction on t (on the lc_at derivation or on t). ✓ Used to prove lc (abs T1 (subst sigma t1)) from lc_at 1 t1 and lc_at 1 (sigma x) for all x. And lc_at 0 (sigma x) → lc_at 1 (sigma x) by monotonicity.
- `lc_at_mono : forall t k k', k <= k' -> lc_at k t -> lc_at k' t`. Induction on t or on lc_at.

**fv**: 
```
Fixpoint fv (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => nil
  | tm_fvar x => [x]
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fv t1 ++ fv t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fv t1
  | tm_true => nil | tm_false => nil
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => fv t1 ++ fv t2 ++ fv t3
  end.
```

**Fresh atom construction**: Need `exists x, ~ In x L /\ ~ In x (fv t1)`. Since atoms = nat and lists are finite:
```
Lemma fresh : forall (L1 L2 : list atom), exists x, ~ In x L1 /\ ~ In x L2.
```
Prove via a max-based construction: use `fold_right max 0 (L1 ++ L2)` and take S of it. Lemmas: `forall L x, In x L -> x <= fold_right max 0 L` — need a list-max lemma. Alternatively use `List.length`? Hmm, since atoms are nat and L1++L2 has finite length, the number length(L1++L2) might be in the list? No: a list of nats of length n could contain n (e.g., [0,1,2] contains 2 = length-1, not 3; actually a list of length n can't contain n if all elements are distinct and in range... but [5,5] has length 2 and contains 5 ≥ 2). Hmm. Max is safer.

Define `max_list : list nat -> nat := fold_right max 0`. Lemma: `In x L -> x <= max_list L` by induction on L. Then fresh x := S (max_list (L1 ++ L2)); if In x (L1++L2) then x <= max_list ≤ S(...) - 1 < x contradiction. Actually x = S m, and In x L → x ≤ max_list L = m → S m ≤ m → contradiction by lia/omega.

**Local closure lemmas:**
- `lc_at_mono : forall k k' t, k <= k' -> lc_at k t -> lc_at k' t`.

  - ST_AppAbs: lc (app (abs T t) v) → lc (abs T t) and lc v → lc_at 1 t and lc_at 0 v → lc (open t v) = lc_at 0 (open_rec 0 v t) ✓ via lc_at_open_rec.

  Wait, but does the induction principle for has_type give the IH with the L-premise? `induction 1` on has_type: for T_Abs the IH is `forall x, ~ In x L -> locally_closed (open t1 (fvar x))`? The T_Abs constructor's argument is `(forall x, ~ In x L -> has_type (x|->T1;Gamma) (open t1 (fvar x)) T2)`. Induction on the derivation: Coq generates the IH for the premise, but since the premise is a `forall` of a function type, the IH will be `forall x, ~ In x L -> <motive applied>`. Actually for a premise of the form `forall x, ~ In x L -> has_type ...`, the induction principle gives IH : `forall x, ~ In x L -> P (open t1 (fvar x)) T2`? Hmm, with nested products as premises, Coq's induction principle puts the whole premise under the IH... Let me think: `Inductive has_type : context -> tm -> ty -> Prop := | T_Abs : forall (L : list atom) Gamma T1 T2 t1, (forall x, ~ In x L -> has_type (update Gamma x T1) (open t1 (tm_fvar x)) T2) -> has_type Gamma (tm_abs T1 t1) (Ty_Arrow T1 T2)`.
  
  The generated induction principle: for P : context -> tm -> ty -> Prop,
  `has_type_ind : forall P, (forall L Gamma T1 T2 t1, (forall x H, P (update Gamma x T1) (open t1 (fvar x)) T2) -> P Gamma (abs T1 t1) (T1->T2)) -> ... -> forall Gamma t T, has_type Gamma t T -> P Gamma t T`.
  
  Hmm, actually for premises that are themselves `forall`-quantified, Coq's induction principle treats the entire premise `forall x, ~ In x L -> has_type ...` as one argument, and gives the IH as `(forall x H, P (update Gamma x T1) (open t1 (fvar x)) T2)` — i.e., the IH is put inside the quantifier. Yes, I believe Coq does this: when the premise is a product type ending in the inductive, it generalizes the IH under the products. So `induction 1` gives IH : forall x, ~ In x L -> locally_closed (open t1 (fvar x)) ✓.

For R_env with update:
```
Lemma R_env_update : forall sigma v Gamma x T1,
  R T1 v -> R_env sigma Gamma -> R_env (upd sigma x v) (update Gamma x T1).
Proof.
  intros sigma v Gamma x T1 Hv Hsig y S HS.
  unfold update in HS. destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E.
  - inversion HS; subst. (* S = T1 *) 
    (* need R T1 (upd sigma x v y) = if eqb x y then v else sigma y = v since eqb x y = true *)
    unfold upd. rewrite E. exact Hv.
  - apply Hsig. exact HS.
Qed.
```
Wait: `inversion HS` where HS : (if (x =? y) then Some T1 else Gamma y) = Some S. With E : (x =? y) = true, HS becomes Some T1 = Some S, so S = T1 ✓.

Note `inversion HS; subst` — need S and T1 to be variables, fine.

Now the R_env for empty context:
```
Lemma R_env_empty : forall sigma, R_env sigma empty.
Proof. intros sigma x T H; unfold empty in H; discriminate. Qed.
```
`empty x` reduces to None, so H : None = Some T, `discriminate` ✓. Might need `simpl in H` or `unfold empty in H`.

Now, let's write `subst`:
```
Fixpoint subst (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => sigma x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst sigma t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => tm_if (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2) (subst sigma t3)
  end.
```
Careful about the Fixpoint argument: `subst sigma t` with sigma first — structural recursion on t ✓ (sigma is not structurally decreasing but t is; Coq should accept since the recursive calls are on subterms of t). `subst_upd_agree`:
```
Definition upd (sigma : atom -> tm) (x : atom) (v : tm) : atom -> tm :=
  fun y => if Nat.eqb x y then v else sigma y.

Lemma subst_upd_agree : forall t x v sigma,
  ~ In x (fv t) -> subst (upd sigma x v) t = subst sigma t.
Proof.
  induction t; simpl; intros; auto.

    + apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst a. simpl in H. contradiction.  (* H : ~ In x [x] *)

```

Now, freshness:
```
Fixpoint max_list (l : list nat) : nat :=
  match l with nil => 0 | x :: xs => max x (max_list xs) end.
```
Then prove `In x l -> x <= max_list l`. Using `Nat.max` lemmas: `Nat.le_max_l`, `Nat.le_max_r`.

Actually easier: use `fold_right max 0 l`? A Fixpoint is clearer.

```
Lemma in_max_list : forall l x, In x l -> x <= max_list l.
Proof.
  induction l; simpl; intros.
  - inversion H.
  - destruct H as [-> | H].
    + apply Nat.le_max_l.
    + apply Nat.le_trans with (max_list l); [apply IHl; exact H | apply Nat.le_max_r].
Qed.

Lemma fresh_two : forall L1 L2, exists x, ~ In x L1 /\ ~ In x L2.
Proof.
  intros. exists (S (max_list (L1 ++ L2))).
  assert (H : forall L, In (S (max_list (L1 ++ L2))) L -> False). ...
Qed.
```
Simpler: prove `forall L, ~ In (S (max_list L)) L`: if In, then S (max_list L) <= max_list L by in_max_list, contradicting `Nat.nle_succ_diag_l`. ✓ Then fresh: x := S (max_list (L1 ++ L2)); ~ In x L1 since In x L1 → In x (L1++L2) → contradiction; similarly L2. ✓

Let me restructure:
```coq
Lemma lc_step : forall t t', locally_closed t -> t --> t' -> locally_closed t'.
Proof.
  intros t t' Hlc Hst. revert Hlc. induction Hst; intros Hlc.
  - (* ST_AppAbs: t = app (abs T t) v *) 
    apply lc_app_inv in Hlc as [Habs Hv].
    apply lc_abs_inv in Habs.

  - (* ST_App1 *) 
    apply lc_app_inv in Hlc as [H1 H2].
    apply lc_app; [apply IHHst; exact H1 | exact H2].
  - (* ST_App2 *) 
    apply lc_app_inv in Hlc as [H1 H2].
    apply lc_app; [exact H1 | apply IHHst; exact H2].
  - (* ST_IfTrue: t' = t1 *) exact (proj1 (proj1? ...)).
    apply lc_if_inv in Hlc as [H1 [H2 H3]]. exact H1.
  - (* ST_IfFalse *) apply lc_if_inv in Hlc as [H1 [H2 H3]]. exact H2.
  - (* ST_If *) apply lc_if_inv in Hlc as [H1 [H2 H3]].
    apply lc_if; [apply IHHst; exact H1 | exact H2 | exact H3].
Qed.
```

```coq
Lemma has_type_lc : forall Gamma t T, has_type Gamma t T -> locally_closed t.
Proof.
  intros Gamma t T H. induction H.
  - (* T_Var *) simpl; apply lc_fvar.
  - (* T_Abs *) simpl; apply lc_abs. 
    (* IH : forall x, ~ In x L -> locally_closed (open t1 (fvar x)) *)
```

```coq
Lemma R_step : forall T t t', R T t -> t --> t' -> R T t'.
Proof.
  induction T; intros t t' HR Hst; simpl in *.
  - (* Bool *) destruct HR as [Hlc Hsn]. split.
    + apply lc_step with (t := t); assumption.
    + apply SN_step with (t := t); assumption.
  - (* Arrow T1 T2 *) destruct HR as [Hlc [Hsn Happ]]. split; [ | split].
    + apply lc_step with (t := t); assumption.
    + apply SN_step with (t := t); assumption.
    + intros u Hu. 
      apply IHT2 with (t := tm_app t u).  (* IHT2 : forall t t', R T2 t -> step t t' -> R T2 t' *)
      * apply Happ. exact Hu.
      * apply ST_App1; [exact Hst | apply R_lc with (T := T1); exact Hu].
Qed.
```
Hmm, `induction T` gives IHT1 : forall t t', R T1 t -> step t t' -> R T1 t' and IHT2 similarly ✓. Note the induction is on ty with the goal `forall t t', R T t -> t --> t' -> R T t'`. Coq's `induction T` will generalize t, t', HR, Hst? Not automatically — the goal mentions t, t', HR, Hst which depend on T only through R T. The motive abstracts T: fun T => forall t t', R T t -> step t t' -> R T t'. Coq should generalize the variables that depend on T. Since t, t' don't depend on T but HR : R T t does... Coq's induction will fail with "cannot eliminate" unless we revert HR (and maybe Hst). Standard practice:
```
  induction T; intros t t' HR Hst; simpl in *.
```
When the goal is `forall t t', R T t -> ...` — wait, the statement is `forall T t t', ...`, so after `intro T` we have T fixed. To do induction on T while t, t', etc. are still universally quantified in the goal, we should do `induction T` right at the start when the goal is `forall T t t', R T t -> t --> t' -> R T t'`. Then the motive is fun T => forall t t', R T t -> step t t' -> R T t' ✓ and Coq handles it fine (the quantified variables are in the goal). So:
```
Lemma R_step : forall T t t', R T t -> t --> t' -> R T t'.
```

```coq
Lemma R_if : forall T b u w,
  R Ty_Bool b -> R T u -> R T w -> R T (tm_if b u w).
Proof.
  intros T b u w Hb Hu Hw.
  pose proof (R_sn _ _ Hb) as Hsn_b.
  revert Hb Hu Hw.
  induction Hsn_b as [b Hstep_b IH]. intros Hb Hu Hw.
  apply (R_neutral T).
  - (* lc *) apply lc_if; [apply R_lc with (T:=Ty_Bool) | apply R_lc with (T:=T) | ...]; assumption.
  - (* neutral *) apply neutral_if.
  - (* SN *) apply SN_intro. intros t'' Hst. apply R_sn with (T := T). (* need the reducts lemma *) 
  - (* reducts *) intros t'' Hst. inversion Hst; subst; clear Hst.
    + (* ST_IfTrue *) exact Hu.
    + (* ST_IfFalse *) exact Hw.
    + (* ST_If *) apply IH; [ apply R_step with (t := b); assumption | exact Hu | exact Hw ].
Qed.
```

```coq
  assert (Hreds : forall t'', tm_if b u w --> t'' -> R T t'').
  { intros t'' Hst. inversion Hst; subst; clear Hst.
    - (* ST_IfTrue: b = tm_true, t'' = t1 *) exact Hu.
    - (* ST_IfFalse *) exact Hw.
    - (* ST_If: b --> t1', t'' = if t1' u t3 *) apply IH; [apply R_step with (t := b); assumption | exact Hu | exact Hw].
  }
  apply (R_neutral T).
  - apply lc_if; [apply R_lc with (T := Ty_Bool); exact Hb | apply R_lc with (T := T); exact Hu | apply R_lc with (T := T); exact Hw].
  - apply neutral_if.
  - apply SN_intro. intros t'' Hst. apply R_sn with (T := T). apply Hreds. exact Hst.
  - exact Hreds.
Qed.
```
Careful with inversion details: Hst : step (tm_if b u w) t''. Constructors:
- ST_IfTrue : forall t1 t2, lc t1 -> lc t2 -> step (if true t1 t2) t1. Unifies b = true, t1 = u, t2 = w, t'' = u. Hypotheses: lc u, lc w.
- ST_IfFalse: b = false, t'' = w.
- ST_If : forall t1 t1' t2 t3, step t1 t1' -> lc t2 -> lc t3 -> step (if t1 t2 t3) (if t1' t2 t3). Unifies t1 = b, t2 = u, t3 = w, t'' = if b' u w. Hypotheses: step b b', lc u, lc w.
So in the ST_If case, IH : forall b', step b b' -> R Bool b' -> R T u -> R T w -> R T (if b' u w). Apply with b' and R_step at Bool ✓.

Great. Note: the induction on Hsn_b with revert Hb Hu Hw: motive fun b => R Bool b -> R T u -> R T w -> R T (if b u w) ✓.

**Fundamental theorem.**


Hmm. That's a real issue: R_env only constrains sigma on the domain of Gamma. So subst sigma t1 could contain arbitrary junk for unconstrained variables. But does that matter? For lc, we need lc_at 1 (subst sigma t1), but only the fvars occurring in t1 matter; t1 might contain fvars not in dom(Gamma)! E.g., in the derivation `empty |-- t \in T`, t is closed... but intermediate contexts? In T_Abs, the body is opened with fvar x and typed under x |-> T1; Gamma. The body could contain other free variables y with Gamma y = Some S. So sigma y is constrained for those. But if t1 contains fvar y where Gamma y = None, then... can that happen in a well-typed term? T_Var requires Gamma x = Some T, so any fvar in a well-typed term is in dom(Gamma) — at the point of use. But the term t1's fvars are exactly those used in the derivation with the ambient context, hence all in dom(Gamma) ✓.

So the issue is that sigma is unconstrained outside dom(Gamma). To prove lc_at 1 (subst sigma t1), we need all fvars of t1 to map to lc terms. Since all fvars of t1 are in dom(Gamma) and R_env constrains them, we're fine — but we need to connect "fv t1 ⊆ dom(Gamma)" to the proof. Hmm, how?

Option A: strengthen R_env to constrain sigma everywhere: define R_env as: forall x, Gamma x = Some T -> R T (sigma x), plus sigma maps everything to lc terms? Alternatively define sigma's images to be... Hmm.

**Main theorem**:
```coq
Theorem normalization : forall t T, <{ empty |-- t \in T }> -> strongly_normalizing t.
Proof.
  intros t T Hty.
  assert (HR : R T t).
  { apply (fundamental empty t T Hty (fun x => tm_fvar x)).
    split.
    - intros x. apply lc_fvar.
    - intros x S HS. unfold empty in HS. discriminate. }
  apply R_sn in HR. exact HR.
Qed.
```
Hmm, `apply (fundamental empty t T Hty (fun x => tm_fvar x))` — fundamental Gamma t T : has_type ... -> forall sigma, R_env sigma Gamma -> R T (subst sigma t). Applying to Hty gives forall sigma, R_env ... -> R T (subst sigma t); then applying to sigma gives R T (subst sigma t) which reduces to R T t (with sigma := fun x => fvar x, subst sigma t = t by subst_fvar_id). The `apply` tactic would need to unify R T (subst (fun x => fvar x) t) with R T t — not definitionally equal, so apply fails. Instead:
```
  assert (HR' : R T (subst (fun x => tm_fvar x) t)) by (apply (fundamental ...); ...).
  rewrite subst_fvar_id in HR'. 
```
✓.

```coq
(** ** Free variables and substitution *)

Fixpoint fv (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => nil
  | tm_fvar x => x :: nil
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fv t1 ++ fv t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fv t1
  | tm_true => nil
  | tm_false => nil
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => fv t1 ++ fv t2 ++ fv t3
  end.

Fixpoint max_list (l : list nat) : nat :=
  match l with
  | nil => 0
  | x :: xs => Nat.max x (max_list xs)
  end.

Lemma in_max_list : forall l x, In x l -> x <= max_list l.
Proof.
  induction l as [| y ys IH]; intros x Hin; simpl in *.
  - inversion Hin.
  - destruct Hin as [-> | Hin].
    + apply Nat.le_max_l.
    + apply Nat.le_trans with (max_list ys); [apply IH; exact Hin | apply Nat.le_max_r].
Qed.

Lemma not_in_max_succ : forall l, ~ In (S (max_list l)) l.
Proof.
  intros l Hin. pose proof (in_max_list l (S (max_list l)) Hin). lia.
Qed.

Lemma fresh_atom : forall L : list atom, exists x, ~ In x L.
Proof. intros L. exists (S (max_list L)). apply not_in_max_succ. Qed.

Lemma fresh_atom_two : forall L1 L2 : list atom, exists x, ~ In x L1 /\ ~ In x L2.
Proof.
  intros L1 L2. exists (S (max_list (L1 ++ L2))). split; intro Hin;
  apply not_in_max_succ with (l := L1 ++ L2); apply in_app_iff; [left | right]; exact Hin.
Qed.
```

```coq
(** ** Auxiliary definitions *)

Fixpoint fv (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => nil
  | tm_fvar x => x :: nil
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fv t1 ++ fv t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fv t1
  | tm_true => nil
  | tm_false => nil
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => fv t1 ++ fv t2 ++ fv t3
  end.

Fixpoint subst (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => sigma x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst sigma t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => tm_if (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2) (subst sigma t3)
  end.

Definition upd (sigma : atom -> tm) (x : atom) (v : tm) : atom -> tm :=
  fun y => if Nat.eqb x y then v else sigma y.

Definition neutral (t : tm) : Prop := ~ value t.

Definition R_env (sigma : atom -> tm) (Gamma : context) : Prop :=
  (forall x, locally_closed (sigma x)) /\
  (forall x T, Gamma x = Some T -> R T (sigma x)).
```

```coq
      locally_closed t /\
      strongly_normalizing t /\
      (forall u, R T1 u -> R T2 (tm_app t u))
  end.
```

```coq
(** STLC call-by-value strong-normalization benchmark task. *)

From Stdlib Require Import Arith.PeanoNat.
From Stdlib Require Import Lists.List.
From Stdlib Require Import Relations.Relation_Definitions.
From Stdlib Require Import Lia.

Module STLCCBVNormalizationTask.

... [original through strongly_normalizing] ...

(** ** Auxiliary development for strong normalization *)

(** Free variables of a term. *)
Fixpoint fv (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => nil
  | tm_fvar x => x :: nil
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fv t1 ++ fv t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fv t1
  | tm_true => nil
  | tm_false => nil
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => fv t1 ++ fv t2 ++ fv t3
  end.

Fixpoint max_list (l : list nat) : nat :=
  match l with
  | nil => 0
  | x :: xs => Nat.max x (max_list xs)
  end.

Lemma in_max_list : forall l x, In x l -> x <= max_list l.
Proof.
  induction l as [| y ys IH]; intros x Hin; simpl in *.
  - inversion Hin.
  - destruct Hin as [-> | Hin].
    + apply Nat.le_max_l.
    + apply Nat.le_trans with (max_list ys); [apply IH; exact Hin | apply Nat.le_max_r].
Qed.

Lemma not_in_max_succ : forall l : list nat, ~ In (S (max_list l)) l.
Proof.
  intros l Hin. pose proof (in_max_list l (S (max_list l)) Hin). lia.
Qed.

Lemma fresh_atom : forall L : list atom, exists x, ~ In x L.
Proof. intros L. exists (S (max_list L)). apply not_in_max_succ. Qed.

Lemma fresh_atom_two : forall L1 L2 : list atom,
  exists x, ~ In x L1 /\ ~ In x L2.
Proof.
  intros L1 L2. exists (S (max_list (L1 ++ L2))). split; intro Hin;
    apply not_in_max_succ with (l := L1 ++ L2); rewrite in_app_iff;
    [left | right]; exact Hin.
Qed.
```

```coq

  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => sigma x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst_tm sigma t1) (subst_tm sigma t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst_tm sigma t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 =>
      tm_if (subst_tm sigma t1) (subst_tm sigma t2) (subst_tm sigma t3)
  end.

Definition upd (sigma : atom -> tm) (x : atom) (v : tm) : atom -> tm :=
  fun y => if Nat.eqb x y then v else sigma y.

Lemma subst_tm_open_rec : forall sigma k u t,
  subst_tm sigma (open_rec k u t) =
  open_rec k (subst_tm sigma u) (subst_tm sigma t).
Proof.
  intros sigma k u t. revert k u.
  induction t as [n | x | t1 IH1 t2 IH2 | T t1 IH1 | | | t1 IH1 t2 IH2 t3 IH3];
    intros k u; simpl.
  - destruct (Nat.eqb k n); reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
  - rewrite IH1, IH2. reflexivity.
  - rewrite IH1. reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
  - rewrite IH1, IH2, IH3. reflexivity.
Qed.
```
Hmm: in the bvar case, after simpl, is the goal `subst_tm sigma (if k =? n then u else tm_bvar n) = (if k =? n then subst_tm sigma u else tm_bvar n)`? `simpl` unfolds `open_rec k u (tm_bvar n)` to `if Nat.eqb k n then u else tm_bvar n`. Then does simpl distribute `subst_tm sigma` over the if? No. So goal is `subst_tm sigma (if k =? n then u else tm_bvar n) = if k =? n then subst_tm sigma u else tm_bvar n`. `destruct (Nat.eqb k n); reflexivity`: in the true branch, goal `subst_tm sigma u = subst_tm sigma u` ✓; false branch `subst_tm sigma (tm_bvar n) = tm_bvar n` ✓ — but does `subst_tm sigma (tm_bvar n)` simplify to `tm_bvar n` definitionally? It's a Fixpoint on a constructor → yes, `reflexivity` should work up to δ/ι reduction ✓. Actually after destruct, the term `if ... then u else tm_bvar n` reduces to `u` or `tm_bvar n` ✓. In the false branch the goal is `subst_tm sigma (tm_bvar n) = tm_bvar n` which is definitionally true ✓.

Hmm, also: did simpl reduce `subst_tm sigma (tm_bvar n)` on the RHS to `tm_bvar n`? Yes if simpl unfolded it ✓.

For the abs case: goal after simpl:
`subst_tm sigma (tm_abs T (open_rec (S k) u t1)) = tm_abs T (open_rec (S k) (subst_tm sigma u) (subst_tm sigma t1))`
→ simpl reduces LHS to `tm_abs T (subst_tm sigma (open_rec (S k) u t1))`. Then rewrite IH1 (with k := S k) gives `tm_abs T (open_rec (S k) (subst_tm sigma u) (subst_tm sigma t1))` ✓ reflexivity.

```coq
Lemma lc_at_mono : forall t k k', k <= k' -> lc_at k t -> lc_at k' t.
```

```coq
Lemma lc_app_inv : forall k t1 t2, lc_at k (tm_app t1 t2) -> lc_at k t1 /\ lc_at k t2.
```

```coq
Lemma lc_at_open_rec : forall t k u,
  lc_at (S k) t -> lc_at k u -> lc_at k (open_rec k u t).
```

```coq
Lemma lc_at_open_down : forall t k u,

  - apply lc_app_inv in H as [H1 H2]. apply lc_app; [exact (IH1 k u H1) | exact (IH2 k u H2)].
  - apply lc_abs. apply lc_abs_inv in H. exact (IH1 (S k) u H).
  - apply lc_true.
  - apply lc_false.
  - apply lc_if_inv in H as [H1 [H2 H3]]. apply lc_if; [exact (IH1 k u H1) | exact (IH2 k u H2) | exact (IH3 k u H3)].
```

`lc_step`:
```coq
Lemma lc_step : forall t t', locally_closed t -> t --> t' -> locally_closed t'.
Proof.
  intros t t' Hlc Hst. revert Hlc.
  induction Hst; intros Hlc.
  - (* ST_AppAbs *) 
    apply lc_app_inv in Hlc as [Habs Hv].
    apply lc_abs_inv in Habs.
    simpl. apply lc_at_open_rec with (k := 0); assumption.
```

```
  - (* ST_App1 *) 
    apply lc_app_inv in Hlc as [H1 H2]. apply lc_app; [apply IHHst; exact H1 | exact H2].
```
Hmm, `induction Hst` with the motive `fun t t' H => locally_closed t -> locally_closed t'` — the IHs would be named IHHst or similar. With `induction Hst`, Coq names the IH after the hypothesis name: `IHHst` ✓ (the hypothesis is named Hst). But in ST_App1 the recursive premise is a step; the IH : locally_closed t1 -> locally_closed t1' ✓.

Hmm, but wait: with `revert Hlc; induction Hst; intros Hlc` — the motive is fun t t' (s : step t t') => locally_closed t -> locally_closed t'. In the ST_App1 case, the IH for the premise `t1 --> t1'` is `locally_closed t1 -> locally_closed t1'` ✓.

  - ST_App2: apply lc_app_inv in Hlc as [H1 H2]; apply lc_app; [exact H1 | apply IHHst; exact H2].
  - ST_IfTrue: apply lc_if_inv in Hlc as [H1 [H2 H3]]; exact H1.
  - ST_IfFalse: exact H2.
  - ST_If: apply lc_if_inv in Hlc as [H1 [H2 H3]]; apply lc_if; [apply IHHst; exact H1 | exact H2 | exact H3].
Qed.

Hmm, careful: in ST_IfTrue, the constructor is `ST_IfTrue : forall t1 t2, locally_closed t1 -> locally_closed t2 -> tm_if tm_true t1 t2 --> t1`. The conclusion is `tm_if tm_true t1 t2 --> t1`. But our induction motive's Hlc is about the *redex*, and in this case the redex is `tm_if tm_true t1 t2`; so Hlc : lc (tm_if tm_true t1 t2) → lc_if_inv gives lc tm_true, lc t1, lc t2 → goal lc t1 ✓ exact H2 (the second component). ✓

Now `has_type_lc`:
```coq
Lemma has_type_lc : forall Gamma t T, has_type Gamma t T -> locally_closed t.
Proof.
  intros Gamma t T Hty. induction Hty as
    [Gamma0 x T0 Hvar
    | L Gamma0 T1 T2 t1 Hbody IHbody
    | Gamma0 t1 t2 T1 T2 H1 IH1 H2 IH2
    | Gamma0 | Gamma0
    | Gamma0 t1 t2 t3 T0 H1 IH1 H2 IH2 H3 IH3].

  - simpl. apply lc_abs.
    destruct (fresh_atom L) as [x Hx].
    specialize (IHbody x Hx).
    exact (lc_at_open_down t1 0 (tm_fvar x) IHbody).
  - simpl. apply lc_app; assumption.
  - simpl. apply lc_true.
  - simpl. apply lc_false.
  - simpl. apply lc_if; assumption.
Qed.
```

Now `SN` lemmas:
```coq
Lemma SN_step : forall t t', strongly_normalizing t -> t --> t' -> strongly_normalizing t'.
Proof. intros t t' Hsn Hst. destruct Hsn as [Hprem]. apply Hprem. exact Hst. Qed.

Lemma value_no_step : forall t t', value t -> ~ (t --> t').
Proof. intros t t' Hv Hst. destruct Hv; inversion Hst. Qed.

Lemma SN_value : forall t, value t -> strongly_normalizing t.
Proof.
  intros t Hv. apply SN_intro. intros t' Hst.
  exfalso. eapply value_no_step; eauto.
Qed.
```
Hmm, `destruct Hsn as [Hprem]` — the SN_intro constructor has one argument (the premise). `destruct Hsn as [Hprem]` should work ✓.

`value_no_step`: `destruct Hv; inversion Hst` — in the v_abs case, Hst : step (tm_abs T t0) t'; inversion Hst should close (no constructor). ✓ but the goal is False. `inversion Hst` on a hypothesis with no matching constructor solves the goal ✓. For v_true/v_false similarly ✓.

Now the R definitions & lemmas. Let me write them:

```coq
Fixpoint R (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => locally_closed t /\ strongly_normalizing t
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      locally_closed t /\
      strongly_normalizing t /\
      (forall u, R T1 u -> R T2 (tm_app t u))
  end.

Definition R_env (sigma : atom -> tm) (Gamma : context) : Prop :=
  (forall x, locally_closed (sigma x)) /\
  (forall x T, Gamma x = Some T -> R T (sigma x)).

Definition neutral (t : tm) : Prop := ~ value t.

Lemma R_lc : forall T t, R T t -> locally_closed t.
Proof. intros T t H; destruct T; simpl in H; tauto. Qed.
```
Hmm: `destruct T` — but T appears in H : R T t. `destruct T` will generalize. Since the goal `locally_closed t` doesn't mention T, Coq might complain about dependent destruction. Standard: `intros T; destruct T; intros t H; simpl in H; tauto` — after intro T and destruct T, the goals are for Bool and Arrow; then intros. ✓ Let me write `intros T; destruct T; intros t H; simpl in H; tauto`.

Hmm, `simpl in H` — H : R Ty_Bool t. `simpl` should unfold R since the Fixpoint's argument is a constructor ✓. Then H : locally_closed t /\ strongly_normalizing t; tauto proves locally_closed t ✓.

Similarly R_sn.

```coq
Lemma R_sn : forall T t, R T t -> strongly_normalizing t.
Proof. intros T; destruct T; intros t H; simpl in H; tauto. Qed.

Lemma R_app : forall T1 T2 t u, R (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) t -> R T1 u -> R T2 (tm_app t u).
Proof. intros T1 T2 t u H Hu. simpl in H. tauto. Qed.
```
Hmm: `simpl in H` where H : R (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) t → unfolds to lc ∧ SN ∧ forall u, R T1 u -> R T2 (app t u). Then `tauto`? The goal is R T2 (app t u) — tauto may not use the forall properly (it's a Prop-level forall; tauto can instantiate? tauto does handle first-order logic with quantifiers, yes, but the hypothesis is `forall u0, R T1 u0 -> R T2 (app t u0)` and we need R T2 (app t u) — tauto should manage since it's Horn-like). To be safe:

```coq
Lemma R_step : forall T t t', R T t -> t --> t' -> R T t'.
```

One more thing: `R_neutral`'s induction on SN u. Let me write it as:
```coq
    pose proof (R_sn T1 u Hu) as Hsn_u.
    revert Hu.
    induction Hsn_u as [u0 Hstep_u IH_u].
    intros Hu.
```
Hmm, `induction Hsn_u` where Hsn_u : SN u — this generalizes u in the goal `R T2 (app t u)`. With `revert Hu` first, the goal is `R T1 u -> R T2 (app t u)`. Then `induction Hsn_u as [u0 Hstep_u IH_u]` — the motive abstracts u: fun u => R T1 u -> R T2 (app t u). The case: for u0, Hstep_u : forall u', step u0 u' -> SN u', IH_u : forall u', step u0 u' -> R T1 u' -> R T2 (app t u'). Then `intros Hu` gives the goal R T2 (app t u0) with Hu : R T1 u0 ✓.

Wait, the SN constructor's premise binder is named t' in the source: `SN_intro : forall t, (forall t', t --> t' -> SN t') -> SN t`. After induction, the premise hypothesis gets a name — I choose with the pattern `[u0 Hstep_u IH_u]`? The induction principle SN_ind : forall P, (forall t, (forall t', step t t' -> P t') -> P t) -> forall t, SN t -> P t. The `induction Hsn_u as [u0 Hstep_u IH_u]` pattern: the constructor has two arguments (t and the premise). Hmm, when destructing/inducing on `SN u`, the case has one constructor with arguments: the term (u0) and the premise (forall t', ...), and Coq's intro pattern for the induction should be `[u0 Hstep_u IH_u]`? For induction, Coq automatically names the IH for recursive occurrences inside the premise: the premise contains `SN t'` occurrences, and Coq generates IH_u as the induction hypothesis for each. The standard pattern is: `induction H as [x IH]` for e.g. `Inductive le : ... | le_n : forall n, le n n | le_S : forall n m, le n m -> le n (S m)`. For SN_intro : forall t, (forall t', step t t' -> SN t') -> SN t, the pattern would be `induction H as [t Hprem IH]`? Hmm, the second argument is a function type containing the recursive occurrence; Coq generalizes: for each recursive occurrence, it creates an IH which is *inside* the premise: the generated case type is `forall t, (forall t', step t t' -> P t' ...)`? 

Actually, when the recursive occurrence is under a binder in a premise (like `forall t', step t t' -> SN t'`), Coq's induction principle for the motive P gives: `forall t, (forall t', step t t' -> P t') -> P t`. So the case has two arguments: t and the function (forall t', step t t' -> P t'). The intro pattern naming them is `[t Hprem]` — the IH is not separately named; it's Hprem with P instead of SN. So `induction Hsn_u as [u0 Hprem]` gives Hprem : forall u', u0 --> u' -> R T1 u' -> R T2 (app t u') ✓ — exactly what I want. Great, so `induction Hsn_u as [u0 Hprem]` and then `intros Hu`.

Hmm, but does Coq complain that the pattern has 2 names but the "real" constructor has 2 arguments ✓. OK.

Let me be careful: sometimes Coq's `induction` with a pattern requires the number of names to match the number of arguments *after* introducing, including IH names automatically inserted. For nested recursive occurrences under binders, Coq does NOT create separate IH names — the motive P is substituted in place. Yes.

Let me write out the whole thing.

```coq
(** ** The logical relation *)

Fixpoint R (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => locally_closed t /\ strongly_normalizing t
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      locally_closed t /\
      strongly_normalizing t /\
      (forall u, R T1 u -> R T2 (tm_app t u))
  end.
```

```coq
Lemma R_neutral : forall T t,
  locally_closed t ->
  neutral t ->
  strongly_normalizing t ->
  (forall t', t --> t' -> R T t') ->
  R T t.
Proof.
  induction T as [| T1 IH1 T2 IH2]; intros t Hlc Hn Hsn Hred; simpl.
  - split; assumption.
  - split; [exact Hlc | split; [exact Hsn |]].
    intros u Hu.
    pose proof (R_sn T1 u Hu) as Hsn_u.
    revert Hu.
    induction Hsn_u as [u0 Hprem].
    intros Hu.
    assert (Hreds_app : forall w, tm_app t u0 --> w -> R T2 w).
    { intros w Hst.
      destruct (step_app_inv t u0 w Hst) as
        [[T0 [b0 [v [Ht [Hu' [Hval Hw]]]]]] | [[t1' [Hst1 Hw]] | [u' [Hst2 Hw]]]].
      - subst t u0 w. exfalso. apply Hn. apply v_abs. exact Hlc.
        (* Hmm: Ht : t = tm_abs T0 b0, Hu' : u0 = v, Hw : w = open b0 v *)

      - subst w. apply Hprem; [exact Hst2 | exact (R_step T1 u0 u' Hu Hst2)].

    apply (IH2 (tm_app t u0)).
    + apply lc_app; [exact Hlc | exact (R_lc T1 u0 Hu)].
    + apply neutral_app.
    + apply SN_intro. intros w Hst. apply R_sn with (T := T2). apply Hreds_app. exact Hst.
    + exact Hreds_app.
Qed.
```

Order of destruct pattern: `destruct (...) as [[T0 [b0 [v [Ht [Hu' [Hval Hw]]]]]] | [[t1' [Hst1 Hw]] | [u' [Hst2 Hw]]]]`. The disjunction: A \/ B \/ C parses as A \/ (B \/ C). So patterns: `[A_pat | [B_pat | C_pat]]`. A = exists T b v, t = abs T b /\ u0 = v /\ value v /\ w = open b v. Pattern: `[T0 [b0 [v [Ht [Hu' [Hval Hw]]]]]]`. Note the conjunction `t = abs T b /\ u0 = v /\ value v /\ w = open b v` parses as `t = abs T b /\ (u0 = v /\ (value v /\ w = open b v))`. So the pattern is `[Ht [Hu' [Hval Hw]]]` ✓.

B = exists t1', t --> t1' /\ w = app t1' u0 → pattern `[t1' [Hst1 Hw]]`.
C = exists u', u0 --> u' /\ w = app t u' → pattern `[u' [Hst2 Hw]]`.

```coq
Lemma R_abs : forall T1 T2 b,
  locally_closed (tm_abs T1 b) ->
  (forall v, value v -> R T1 v -> R T2 (open b v)) ->
  R (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) (tm_abs T1 b).

  intros T1 T2 b Hlc Hbody. simpl. split; [exact Hlc | split].
  - apply SN_value. apply v_abs. exact Hlc.
  - intros u Hu.
    pose proof (R_sn T1 u Hu) as Hsn_u.
    revert Hu.
    induction Hsn_u as [u0 Hprem].
    intros Hu.
    assert (Hreds_app : forall w, tm_app (tm_abs T1 b) u0 --> w -> R T2 w).
    { intros w Hst.
      destruct (step_app_inv (tm_abs T1 b) u0 w Hst) as
        [[T0 [b0 [v [Ht [Hu' [Hval Hw]]]]]] | [[t1' [Hst1 Hw]] | [u' [Hst2 Hw]]]].

        (* Ht : tm_abs T1 b = tm_abs T0 b0 → T1 = T0, b = b0 *)

      - subst w. apply Hprem; [exact Hst2 | exact (R_step T1 u0 u' Hu Hst2)].
    }
    apply (R_neutral T2).
    + apply lc_app; [exact Hlc | exact (R_lc T1 u0 Hu)].
    + apply neutral_app.
    + apply SN_intro. intros w Hst. apply R_sn with (T := T2). apply Hreds_app. exact Hst.
    + exact Hreds_app.
Qed.
```

```coq
Lemma R_if : forall T b u w,
  R Ty_Bool b -> R T u -> R T w -> R T (tm_if b u w).
Proof.
  intros T b u w Hb Hu Hw.
  pose proof (R_sn Ty_Bool b Hb) as Hsn_b.
  revert Hb Hu Hw.
  induction Hsn_b as [b0 Hprem].
  intros Hb Hu Hw.
  assert (Hreds : forall t'', tm_if b0 u w --> t'' -> R T t'').
  { intros t'' Hst.
    destruct (step_if_inv b0 u w t'' Hst) as [[Hb' Ht''] | [[Hb' Ht''] | [b' [Hst' Ht'']]]].
    - subst. exact Hu.
    - subst. exact Hw.
    - subst. apply Hprem; [exact Hst' | exact (R_step Ty_Bool b0 b' Hb Hst') | exact Hu | exact Hw].
  }
  apply (R_neutral T).
  - apply lc_if; [exact (R_lc Ty_Bool b0 Hb) | exact (R_lc T u Hu) | exact (R_lc T w Hw)].
  - apply neutral_if.
  - apply SN_intro. intros t'' Hst. apply R_sn with (T := T). apply Hreds. exact Hst.
  - exact Hreds.
Qed.
```
Careful: in the first disjunct, Hb' : b0 = tm_true and Ht'' : t'' = u. `subst` — b0 is the variable (from the induction) — hmm, after induction the variable is b0, so `subst` rewrites b0 := tm_true ✓ and t'' := u ✓. Goal R T u = Hu ✓.

```coq
Definition R_env (sigma : atom -> tm) (Gamma : context) : Prop :=
  (forall x, locally_closed (sigma x)) /\
  (forall x T, Gamma x = Some T -> R T (sigma x)).

Lemma R_env_empty : forall sigma, R_env sigma empty.
Proof.
  intros sigma. split.
  - intros x. (* need locally_closed (sigma x) — NOT provable! *)
```
Oops! R_env_empty is not provable with the lc requirement! The empty context gives no constraint on sigma, but we still need lc (sigma x) for all x. In the main theorem, we choose sigma := fun x => tm_fvar x, which is lc ✓. But R_env_empty as stated for arbitrary sigma is false.

So instead, in the main theorem, prove R_env (fun x => fvar x) empty directly:
```coq

  { split.
    - intros x. apply lc_fvar.
    - intros x S HS. unfold empty in HS. discriminate. }
```

```coq
Lemma R_env_update : forall sigma Gamma x T1 v,
  R T1 v -> R_env sigma Gamma -> R_env (upd sigma x v) (update Gamma x T1).
Proof.
  intros sigma Gamma x T1 v HRv [Hlc Hsem]. split.
  - intros y. unfold upd. destruct (Nat.eqb x y).
    + apply R_lc with (T := T1). exact HRv.
    + apply Hlc.
  - intros y S HS. unfold update in HS. destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E.
    + inversion HS; subst. unfold upd. rewrite E. exact HRv.
    + apply Hsem. exact HS.
Qed.
```

```coq
Lemma subst_tm_lc_at : forall sigma t k,
  (forall x, lc_at k (sigma x)) -> lc_at k t -> lc_at k (subst_tm sigma t).
Proof.
  intros sigma t. induction t as [n | x | t1 IH1 t2 IH2 | T t1 IH1 | | | t1 IH1 t2 IH2 t3 IH3];
    intros k Hsig Hlc; simpl.
  - exact Hlc.
  - apply Hsig.
  - apply lc_app_inv in Hlc as [H1 H2]. apply lc_app; [exact (IH1 k Hsig H1) | exact (IH2 k Hsig H2)].
  - apply lc_abs. apply lc_abs_inv in Hlc. apply (IH1 (S k)).
    + intros x. apply lc_at_mono with (k := k); [lia | apply Hsig].
    + exact Hlc.
  - apply lc_true.
  - apply lc_false.
  - apply lc_if_inv in Hlc as [H1 [H2 H3]]. apply lc_if; [exact (IH1 k Hsig H1) | exact (IH2 k Hsig H2) | exact (IH3 k Hsig H3)].
Qed.
```

```coq
Theorem fundamental : forall Gamma t T,
  has_type Gamma t T ->
  forall sigma, R_env sigma Gamma -> R T (subst_tm sigma t).
Proof.
  intros Gamma t T Hty. induction Hty as
    [Gamma0 x T0 Hvar
    | L Gamma0 T1 T2 t1 Hbody IHbody
    | Gamma0 t1 t2 T1 T2 H1 IH1 H2 IH2
    | Gamma0 | Gamma0
    | Gamma0 t1 t2 t3 T0 H1 IH1 H2 IH2 H3 IH3];
    intros sigma Hsig; simpl.
  - apply (proj2 Hsig). exact Hvar.
  - apply R_abs.
    + (* lc *) apply lc_abs. apply subst_tm_lc_at with (k := 1).
      * intros x. apply lc_at_mono with (k := 0); [lia | apply (proj1 Hsig)].
      * (* lc_at 1 t1 *)
        pose proof (has_type_lc _ _ _ (T_Abs L Gamma0 T1 T2 t1 Hbody)) as Hlca.
        apply lc_abs_inv in Hlca. exact Hlca.
    + (* body *) intros v Hval HRv.
      destruct (fresh_atom_two L (fv t1)) as [x [HxL Hxfv]].
      specialize (IHbody x HxL).
      specialize (IHbody (upd sigma x v)).
      assert (Heq : subst_tm (upd sigma x v) (open t1 (tm_fvar x)) = open (subst_tm sigma t1) v).
      { unfold open. rewrite subst_tm_open_rec.
        assert (Hx : subst_tm (upd sigma x v) (tm_fvar x) = v).
        { simpl. unfold upd. rewrite Nat.eqb_refl. reflexivity. }
        rewrite Hx.
        rewrite (subst_tm_upd_agree t1 x v sigma Hxfv).
        reflexivity. }
      rewrite <- Heq.
      apply IHbody.
      apply R_env_update; assumption.
  - apply (R_app T1 T2). apply (IH1 sigma Hsig). apply (IH2 sigma Hsig).
  - simpl. split; [apply lc_true | apply SN_value; apply v_true].
  - simpl. split; [apply lc_false | apply SN_value; apply v_false].
  - apply R_if; [apply (IH1 sigma Hsig) | apply (IH2 sigma Hsig) | apply (IH3 sigma Hsig)].
Qed.
```
